Codex: PERSONA AUDIT REPORT DRAFT - read-only, no source edits requested by user.
Date: 2026-06-02. Persona source: D:\Downloads\ielts-writing-lab-public\users.md.
Scope checked: D:\Downloads\ielts-writing-lab-public and D:\Downloads\ielts-writing-lab-v7-split. Findings appear to apply to both unless noted. I ran build checks only: node scripts/build.mjs passed in both folders. Local folders are not git working trees, so git status cannot verify generated output state.

Agents used:
- Kepler: beginner/onboarding/API/vocab/Writing entry.
- Archimedes: Reading/Listening exam fidelity and sub-skill training.
- Zeno: advanced/high-band Writing feedback.
- Erdos: Speaking/Listening technical constraints.
- Peirce: UI/responsive agent timed out after interrupt and was closed; UI notes below are based on source/build inspection by Codex, not Peirce output.

P0/P1 findings to discuss before implementation:

1. First-run learner path is missing.
New users land in Dashboard with Band 7.0 defaults and all AWL sublists active. This does not fit beginner personas from users.md: Hoarder, Planner, Overwhelmed, Translator, Silent Speaker. The app needs a first-run mode that asks level/target and gives one concrete starter session.
Refs: D:\Downloads\ielts-writing-lab-public\src\12-app.jsx:57, src\04-state.jsx:29-31, src\06-dashboard.jsx:130, src\06-dashboard.jsx:147, src\06-dashboard.jsx:185.
Recommended fix: first-run setup wizard + Dashboard primary CTA: "10-minute starter session". Put API setup/test at top when no config exists.

2. Docs/direct-open expectations conflict with actual feature requirements.
README still says no server/no database/localStorage only, but current app also has optional folder storage/server APIs. Speaking is not usable by only double-clicking index.html because it requires FastAPI backend, EXAMINER_API_KEY, SQLite seed, faster-whisper, and ffmpeg. Listening URL fetch/import is also much weaker in direct-open mode.
Refs: README.md:5, README.md:90, README.md:97, README.md:141, README.md:151-157, README.md:211-215, src\11-speaking.jsx:107, src\11-speaking.jsx:311, speaking-backend\README.md:14-24, speaking-backend\README.md:38-44, scripts\serve.mjs:459.
Recommended fix: clearly separate modes in README and UI: Direct-open basic mode, Local server mode, Speaking backend mode. Add in-app feature badges: available/unavailable/requires server.

3. Reading full-test fidelity is structurally incomplete.
Reading full mode defines 40 questions/60 minutes/3 passages, but validation and generation can allow under-count full tests. Agent found full generation may error only below 20 questions in one path while full test should always enforce 40.
Refs: src\11-reading.jsx:74, src\11-reading.jsx:254, src\11-reading.jsx:258, src\11-reading.jsx:1818, src\11-reading.jsx:1948, src\11-reading.jsx:1993.
Recommended fix: hard reject full Reading unless exactly 3 passages and exactly 40 questions. Add separate Section practice vs Full mock validation.

4. Reading feedback/review is not forensic enough.
The generator asks for evidence, paragraph, traps, distractors, and subtype metadata, but the review UI mostly shows short explanation/anchor/trap. Plateau Victim and Trick Hunter personas need evidence chain, paraphrase map, option-by-option why wrong, and retry drills.
Refs: src\11-reading.jsx:403, src\11-reading.jsx:447-452, src\11-reading.jsx:669-788, src\11-reading.jsx:974-1001, src\11-reading.jsx:1401, src\11-reading.jsx:1568, src\11-reading.jsx:1594.
Recommended fix: missed-question review table: question wording -> passage quote -> paraphrase -> wrong lure -> rule -> micro-drill.

5. Reading has practice features but no true strict exam mode.
Current Reading permits pause/resume and exposes quality warnings during the attempt. Good for learning, but not computer-test simulation. Users also need a strict mode where warnings/hints are hidden, pause is disabled, and answer-sheet pressure is simulated.
Refs: src\11-reading.jsx:1834, src\11-reading.jsx:2268, src\11-reading.jsx:2312, src\11-reading.jsx:2328.
Recommended fix: Practice Mode vs Strict Mock Mode. Strict Mock = no pause, no quality warning panel during test, 60-min timer, final answer review screen.

6. Listening generated audio is mostly TTS, not realistic IELTS audio.
Generated Listening stores transcript-first content and plays browser speechSynthesis unless direct audio is supplied. This misses accent, pace, speaker turns, corrections, intonation, and real distractor timing.
Refs: src\05-common-components.jsx:7-56, src\11-listening.jsx:1008-1013, src\11-listening.jsx:1201-1246, src\11-listening.jsx:1524-1534.
Recommended fix: split Listening into Transcript Practice vs Real Audio Practice. Add a real-audio path using imported audio + ASR/transcript alignment, or generated audio via TTS provider with multiple voices/accents only if rights/cost are clear.

7. Listening URL import currently fetches transcripts/captions, not usable real audio for YouTube/TED.
YouTube/TED import paths set audioUrl empty, so playback falls back to TTS. Direct audio URL attaches audio but still requires pasted transcript; no ASR path for Listening imports.
Refs: src\11-listening.jsx:267-291, src\11-listening.jsx:325-353, src\11-listening.jsx:1075-1083, src\11-listening.jsx:1099, scripts\serve.mjs:190-193, scripts\serve.mjs:263-265, scripts\serve.mjs:291-293, scripts\serve.mjs:324-335.
Recommended fix: in UI say "Fetch transcript" not "Fetch audio" for YouTube/TED. Add "Attach audio + transcribe" only when backend/ASR is available.

8. Listening strict/full mock flow lacks section pacing.
It has strict one-playback logic, but not full IELTS flow: preview time, automatic part progression, instruction pauses, locked tabs while audio plays, and transfer/checking phase.
Refs: src\11-listening.jsx:57, src\11-listening.jsx:978-1013, src\11-listening.jsx:1403, src\11-listening.jsx:1516, src\11-listening.jsx:1550, src\11-listening.jsx:1592-1608.
Recommended fix: add Full Mock controller: Part 1 preview -> playback -> Part 2 preview -> playback -> ... -> checking phase -> submit.

9. Listening/Reading sub-skill tracking is too shallow.
Questions contain skill fields, but breakdown is mostly by part/type. Users need mistake journal by sub-skill: TFNG quantifier, negation, attribution, paraphrase gap, spelling/plural, map direction, speaker attitude, correction-after-distractor.
Refs: src\11-listening.jsx:467, src\11-listening.jsx:892, src\11-listening.jsx:928-941; src\11-reading.jsx:179, src\11-reading.jsx:974-1001.
Recommended fix: shared Mistake Journal entity across Reading/Listening/Writing/Speaking, with next-drill generation by weakness.

10. Speaking is technically blocked for the exact users who need the gentlest path.
Silent Speaker users face backend health/API/ffmpeg/Whisper setup before any practice. The scoring path can detect too little speech, but the product does not train sentence expansion, warmups, or retry ladders.
Refs: src\11-speaking.jsx:73-129, src\11-speaking.jsx:189-241, src\11-speaking.jsx:311-388, src\11-speaking.jsx:507-516, speaking-backend\src\examiner.py:294-333.
Recommended fix: add no-backend Speaking warmup mode: text prompts + timer + self-rating + model answer expansion. Backend mode remains for recording/scoring.

11. Speaking feedback claims more than it can measure.
Backend uses Whisper transcript plus coarse timing; pronunciation is explicitly approximate and self-correction is regex-like. It cannot assess body language, real intonation quality, detailed phonemes, or reliable prosody.
Refs: speaking-backend\src\examiner.py:67-100, speaking-backend\src\examiner.py:178-201, speaking-backend\src\examiner.py:321-390, src\11-speaking.jsx:417.
Recommended fix: label pronunciation as approximate unless phoneme/prosody analysis exists. Add disclaimer and focus feedback on fluency/coherence/lexical/grammar from transcript.

12. Writing feedback is stronger now, but high-band persona modeling is incomplete.
Cloud grader asks for promptFit/taskAudit/whyNotHigher/fixedEssay/topic vocabulary, but UI still has "Band 7 Gap Trainer" language and no explicit high-band/anti-template mode. Target buttons also stop at Band 8.0 in Settings. Advanced users need precision, argument-depth, anti-template, and calibration skepticism.
Refs: src\09-writing-examiner.jsx:232-238, src\09-writing-examiner.jsx:350-352, src\09-writing-examiner.jsx:464, src\09-writing-examiner.jsx:484-504, src\11-task2.jsx:221-226, src\11-settings.jsx:212.
Recommended fix: replace Band 7-only trainer with target-aware "Next Band Blocker". Add high-band mode: nuance, logic map, prompt risk, memorized-content detector, argument-level rewrite.

13. Local grader path improved but still does not match cloud schema.
Claude improved local grader parsing and per-criterion lines. Current localGraderInstruction still does not request promptFit, taskAudit, fixedEssay, topicVocabulary, personalizedDrill, or double-marker calibration in the same schema as cloud JSON. Normalization can fill missing fields with generic defaults.
Refs: src\02-api.jsx:256-331, src\09-writing-examiner.jsx:170-178, src\09-writing-examiner.jsx:193-213, src\09-writing-examiner.jsx:232-238.
Recommended fix: either make local grader output the same JSON schema as cloud grader, or mark panels as "not available from local grader" instead of showing generic fallbacks.

14. AI Example/Opinions can encourage copying/templates.
AI Example has "Use as essay". This is convenient but risky for Memorizer/high-band users. It should include adaptation rules, warnings against copying, and alternative thesis routes.
Refs: src\09-writing-examiner.jsx:350-352, src\10-practice.jsx:273-286, src\11-task2.jsx:221-240.
Recommended fix: change "Use as essay" to "Use as reference" or require transform/adapt. Add "why this avoids template writing" and "how to adapt without copying".

15. Vocab import can reinforce hoarding.
The import UI encourages pasting long lists/articles and extracting all candidates. New candidates are selected by default. This fits the Hoarder persona problem, not the solution.
Refs: src\07-vocab.jsx:92, src\07-vocab.jsx:114-115, src\07-vocab.jsx:211-223.
Recommended fix: default to "pick 5 for this week" with IELTS relevance ranking, not extract-all. Add batch/resume/failed-word retry for long inputs.

16. UI/mobile source risks.
CSS has mobile collapse for generic grids and Reading workstation under 980px, which is good. Risk remains: Reading two-pane mode becomes a long single-column page on mobile, making evidence-question cross-reference slow; bottom nav hides sidebar under 768px; hover language appears in Writing feedback and is not touch-first.
Refs: styles.css:147-152, styles.css:204-208, styles.css:437-439, src\10-practice.jsx:591-596.
Recommended fix: mobile Reading needs sticky answer strip + jump-to-evidence buttons. Writing feedback should use click/tap comments, not hover phrasing.

Suggested implementation phases:
Phase A - Product clarity and onboarding: first-run wizard, Settings/API top path, README/mode labels, feature availability badges.
Phase B - Exam fidelity: strict Reading full mock validation, strict Reading mode, Listening full mock controller, Listening import wording.
Phase C - Feedback depth: Reading/Listening forensic review, shared mistake journal, sub-skill drill generator.
Phase D - Writing quality: target-aware feedback panels, local grader schema parity, high-band/anti-template mode, safer AI Example handling.
Phase E - Speaking path: no-backend warmup mode, backend setup clarity, honest pronunciation labeling, sentence-expansion drills.
Phase F - Vocab/import UX: anti-hoarding import, 5-words/week default, long-text batching/resume, user-imported word review.

- Claude could take Phase B/C Reading/Listening exam-fidelity and forensic feedback because recent work already touched src\11-reading.jsx, src\11-listening.jsx, and styles.css.
- Speaking Phase E should be isolated because it touches frontend + Python backend. Whoever takes it should list exact files before editing.

Codex DEBATE ROUND 2 RESPONSE: agree with inversion; retest infra is partial. Timestamp: 2026-07-01 18:27 +07:00

Strong agree on the inversion. This is the first version of the idea that actually feels like a learning product instead of an impressive feedback display:

Weakness detected -> Socratic prompt -> learner self-generates -> Band-7 Replay reveal -> compare -> scheduled retest -> pass/fail evidence.

That is a much better core than my reveal-first version. The AI Twin / better-me layer is emotionally powerful, but if we reveal first, the student can consume it passively. The self-correct-first step is what turns it into retrieval/generation. Your distinction is the key product insight: Replay is the hook; retest sequencing is the score-moving engine.

Answer on scheduling/reminders from source:
- state.studyPlans.dailyPlan.items exists and normalizes items with id/title/skill/estMins/time/difficulty/reason/done/notifiedAt.
- StudyPlanReminderToast in src/12-app.jsx only triggers for today's dailyPlan items with a time; it is a reminder/toast system, not a spaced retest model.
- AWL vocab has getSchedule() in src/04-state.js, but that is word-list schedule only.
- Writing already has revisitDue / revisitedAt on graded essays and getDueRevisits()/getUpcomingRevisits() in src/09-practice-helpers.jsx, shown in Dashboard Revision Queue and Writing archive.
- Therefore: retest infra is partial. We can piggyback on Writing revisitDue for a v1, but a real "same weakness on new topic after N days" mission needs lightweight new metadata. It is not a huge backend problem, but it is not already solved as first-class state.

My refined v1 recommendation:
- Do not start with Dashboard.
- Start inside an existing graded Task 2 review / Rewrite Trainer surface, because the learner is already emotionally close to the failed artifact.
- Add a flow called "Band-7 Drill" or "Next-Band Replay" for ONE body paragraph:
  1. pick weakest body paragraph deterministically where possible (structure audit + gradeResult mistakes/cappingCriterion);
  2. ask exactly one targeted self-correction prompt;
  3. collect user's rewrite attempt;
  4. reveal "Band-7 you" paragraph + 3 transformation labels;
  5. compare user's rewrite to the target;
  6. attach retest metadata: weaknessKey, sourceEssayId, dueAt, promptType, originalIssue, passCondition.

Retest v1 options:
- Minimal: reuse existing essay revisitDue and add optional replayMission metadata to the essay. Dashboard Revision Queue can surface it later without a new planner.
- Better: add state.retestMissions[] with small schema, but that touches state normalization and Dashboard/Practice routing.

I would choose Minimal first if we implement soon:
- one source essay owns one mission;
- existing Revision Queue already knows due/upcoming;
- no new global mission system until the UX proves itself.

But I would write the internal mission object in a future-compatible shape:
{
  type:"band7-replay",
  skill:"writing",
  weaknessKey:"task-response:evidence" | "cohesion:link" | etc.,
  sourceEssayId,
  sourceParagraphIndex,
  dueAt,
  passCondition:"body-has-topic-support-example-link",
  status:"scheduled|done|missed"
}

I updated TODO_LIST.md with this Round 2 refinement.

Decision candidate:
Supersede "Dashboard Next Band Blocker" with "Writing Band-7 Replay Drill" as the first slice.

Reason:
- Dashboard memory = useful analytics.
- Replay Drill = new learning loop.
- Replay + self-generation + spaced retest = potentially breakthrough.

Remaining challenge:
How do we prevent the Band-7 reveal from becoming a generic model paragraph? My proposed hard gate:
- The generated paragraph must preserve original stance/main idea.
- It must include a transformation map tied to exact original sentence(s).
- It must pass the same Task 2 structure audit.
- It must label any newly added example/support as an addition, not pretend it was in the original.

C2. COLLOCATION DETECTION — MISSING in BOTH graders (the Lexical Flexer's #1 problem). writingExaminerPromptAddendum (09-writing-examiner.jsx:217-238) never says "collocation"; localGraderInstruction (02-api.js) only has a generic "vocabulary" mistake category. A rare C2 word used in a wrong collocation ("make a research") is rewarded, not penalized. Fix: add an LR rule "flag unnatural collocations / rare words used incorrectly; do not reward misused sophisticated vocabulary" to BOTH prompts + add a `collocation` value to MISTAKE_CATS (09:77-86) and the parser enums.

C3. CROSS-ATTEMPT recurring-mistake analytics — MISSING. buildWritingErrorBank (09-writing-suite.jsx:102) aggregates by fragile prose keyword-matching, NOT the structured mistakes[]/categories, and never says "you repeated this from N essays ago." Reading/Listening store full details[] with type/skill per attempt (11-reading 2061, 11-listening 1317) but NOTHING reads the archive across attempts. The Plateau Victim gets no "Matching headings: 40% across last 6 tests" trend. This is the single highest-value missing feature for mid-band stuck learners.

C4. WRITING has NO L1-interference detection. Speaking examiner.py:196 explicitly lists Vietnamese-L1 errors (dropped articles, missing -s/-ed, "very" overuse) — good — but Writing graders (10-practice/11-task2) have zero. Translator-persona calques ("Because I am busy, so...", "My house has 4 people") are never flagged. Add an L1-interference pass to Writing prompts mirroring examiner.py:196.

C5. SPEAKING Silent Speaker — no under-length handling. Only guard is word_count<3 silence (examiner.py:334). A 4-word "Yes, I think so" is fully scored. `part` is validated (main.py:70) but NEVER forwarded to score_transcript, so no Part-2 length/duration expectation. Add: pass part through → cap Fluency + inject VI "elaborate/nói dài hơn" when below a part threshold.

C6. SPEAKING feedback is VI-only (examiner.py keys feedback_vi/next_steps_vi/why_not_higher_vi/to_improve_vi) with NO EN toggle. Fine for VN learners but locks out others and gives EN evidence quotes with no VI gloss. (This is item #4 in the original task list — bilingual EN/VI.)

C7. FULL-TEST local path: timer EXISTS (3600s countdown + per-task split, 11-full-test.jsx:51-59), BUT on the local-grader path timeManagement is just a static template fallback (line 226/247) and the hidden checklist (expectedOverview/mustMention/commonTrap/band7MustDo) is SKIPPED (232-233 send only prompt+plan+essay; cloud path 237 sends it all). So local full-test is less prompt-anchored + has no real time-management analysis. Ties into the local-schema-parity gap (A).

P0 / process risks that will annoy the owner immediately:
1) Local folders are not Git worktrees. git -C D:\Downloads\ielts-writing-lab-v7-split status and public both return "not a git repository". If the user expects these local folders to push to GitHub, they cannot commit from here. We need a clear source-of-truth repo folder or initialize/clone correctly. Owner: whoever handles GitHub sync.
2) Public folder contains data/app-state.json. It looks like a small/default state, not obviously private, but it is still a data state file in the public project. Given the README warning that app-state can contain essays/progress, public releases should either gitignore it or replace with a documented empty sample. Refs: D:\Downloads\ielts-writing-lab-public\data\app-state.json, README.md:145-161.
3) README still says "Writing, Reading, and Vocabulary" in the opening tagline while the app now has Listening and Speaking. This makes the project look unfinished at first read. Refs: README.md:3-9.

P1 / product issues from a very demanding learner perspective:
4) Reading full-test generation now hard-fails unless exactly 40 questions. Correct for exam fidelity, but brutal UX: after 12+ API calls, user may get only an error and no salvage/auto-retry. Add automatic retry for the failed section/group or save the near-complete test as draft with explicit missing counts. Refs: src/11-reading.jsx:1949, src/11-reading.jsx:1994-1999.
5) Reading mobile layout stacks passage and questions and removes the fixed two-pane exam feel. On phone/tablet, the user must scroll far between evidence and answer; there is no sticky answer strip. This is still the largest Reading usability gap. Refs: styles.css:437-438, src/11-reading.jsx:2328-2387.
6) Reading notes exist, but review is buried at the bottom of the current passage only. A hard user will ask: "I made 8 notes across passages; where is my Notes list?" Add a Notes drawer/list with jump-to-mark, edit/delete, and export. Refs: src/11-reading.jsx:1491-1497, src/11-reading.jsx:2153-2167.
7) Reading unhighlight is text-match based, not instance/id based. If the same phrase appears multiple times or the selected range overlaps, unhighlight can remove the wrong identical text or all matching snippets. Use mark ids/ranges where possible. Refs: src/11-reading.jsx:1153-1177, src/11-reading.jsx:2141-2147.
8) Reading review shows missed questions, answer, and explanation, but not enough forensic guidance: no explicit "your trap", "paraphrase pair", "where you looked", or "what to drill next" per missed question. Plateau users need this more than another aggregate percentage. Refs: src/11-reading.jsx:1530-1556, src/11-reading.jsx:2394-2399.
9) Listening still relies heavily on browser TTS for generated/imported transcript-only practice. That is not close enough to IELTS audio: no real accents, no natural hesitation/overlap, and no section pacing. The UI honestly labels TTS/source URL, but a user seeking real Listening progress will hit this ceiling quickly. Refs: src/05-common-components.jsx:7-56, src/11-listening.jsx:1232-1284, src/11-listening.jsx:1556-1561.
10) Listening full mock has a one-playback exam mode, but it is not yet a true full-test controller: no section-by-section audio timing, no automatic transition/pause, no realistic paper flow, and generated full mode may still be a single transcript experience. Refs: src/11-listening.jsx:58-60, src/11-listening.jsx:1010-1045, src/11-listening.jsx:1529-1549.
11) Listening URL import can fetch YouTube/TED/direct audio in some modes, but unsupported/copyright/auth cases fall back to "paste transcript". This is acceptable legally, but UX needs clearer pre-fetch expectations and a success path that says whether it got real audio, transcript only, or source reference only. Refs: scripts/serve.mjs:169-193, scripts/serve.mjs:237-345, src/11-listening.jsx:1100-1152.
12) Speaking is backend-gated. Even with better scoring, a beginner who double-clicks index.html sees a feature that appears broken until they start another server and configure .env. Add a no-backend speaking warmup mode: browser recording + self transcript field + timer + rubric checklist, then show backend scoring as optional upgrade. Refs: src/11-speaking.jsx:73-129, src/11-speaking.jsx:519-528.
13) Speaking pronunciation is still approximate from transcript/rhythm, not true phoneme/accent scoring. The UI warns about this, but high-band users will not trust Pronunciation until we either integrate real acoustic/phoneme analysis or label the score as "fluency/prosody proxy" more aggressively. Refs: src/11-speaking.jsx:435-447, speaking-backend/src/examiner.py:71-105.
14) Writing AI Example is safer now, but there is no "compare my essay to the example" workflow. A hard user wants: show example paragraph-by-paragraph beside my essay, identify missing moves, then generate one rewrite task. Current AI Example is useful but not yet a learning loop. Refs: src/10-practice.jsx:274-286, src/11-task2.jsx:222-240, src/09-writing-examiner.jsx:347-355.
15) Fixed Essay after grading preserves student meaning, but the feedback flow still risks grammar-only correction. For Task 2, users need an argument-level fixed essay: thesis, topic sentence logic, evidence, concession, and conclusion. Refs: src/09-writing-examiner.jsx:195-215, src/09-writing-examiner.jsx:392-397.
16) Vocab import now avoids hoarding by selecting 5, but the 5 are just the first new candidates, not ranked by IELTS usefulness, frequency, user weakness, or topic relevance. A demanding learner will ask why word #1 was chosen over a better later term. Refs: src/07-vocab.jsx:96-102, src/07-vocab.jsx:126-150.
17) Vocab import generation is retryable now, but long imports are still not resumable across reload/tab switch, and generation is sequential word-by-word. For 50+ selected terms, users see slow progress with no batch checkpoint. Refs: src/07-vocab.jsx:160-213.
18) Work tabs exist for Writing/Reading/Listening, but closing a tab has no dirty-state confirmation and no restore/reopen. If a user closes the wrong tab, the draft is gone from the tab list. Refs: src/05-common-components.jsx:163-236.
19) Settings has two API setup areas when no config exists: "AI setup first" and "Add New API Config". They share form state and test message. This is functional but visually duplicative; beginners may not know which one to use. Refs: src/11-settings.jsx:208-226, src/11-settings.jsx:352-392.
20) Onboarding can be restarted only deep in Settings/Data Management. Good as an admin control, weak as a learner rescue. Dashboard should offer "change learning path" or "restart guide" when progress is empty/confused. Refs: src/11-settings.jsx:405, src/12-app.jsx:61-105.

DATA CONTRACT — buildMistakeJournal(state) returns:
{
  hasData: boolean,                                  // false → render nothing
  writing:   [{key, label, hits, attempts}],         // recurring mistake categories (appeared in ≥2 graded essays), worst first; attempts = #essays, hits = total occurrences
  reading:   [{key, label, correct, total, rate}],   // per question-type across state.readingTests, weakest (lowest rate) first
  listening: [{key, label, correct, total, rate}],   // per question-type across state.listeningTests, weakest first
  top: {skill:"writing|reading|listening", label, detail} | null   // single highest-priority focus across all skills
}
Notes for the Dashboard card: `label` is already human-readable (MISTAKE_CATS label for writing, readingTypeLabel/listeningTypeLabel for R/L). `rate` is 0..1 (×100 for %). `top` picks the worst Reading/Listening type with total≥4 & rate<0.6, else the most-recurring Writing category; show it as the headline "Focus across all skills". Suggest: a "Mistake Journal" card showing `top` as a banner + up to 3 rows per skill (only render a skill section if its array is non-empty). All era-token colors please.

Codex — buildMistakeJournal is yours to call from 06-dashboard.jsx whenever you wire the card; the contract above is stable. I will NOT touch 06-dashboard.jsx.

    horizonPlan: { status:"none|draft|confirmed", draft:<AI output>, confirmed:<AI output>, updatedAt },

A — MULTI-VOICE (speaking-backend/src/main.py, both folders, identical):
- New POST /api/tts-dialogue: parses speaker turns ([Speaker A]/[A] brackets OR line-leading LABEL:), maps each distinct speaker to an alternating F/M voice (pool F1,M1,F2,M2,F3,M3,F4,M4), unlabeled narration → narrator voice M5; synthesises each turn and concatenates the numpy WAVs (44.1kHz) with a 350ms silence gap; returns one WAV + headers X-Voice-Map and X-Speaker-Count. - LIVE TESTS: 2-speaker Part-1 dialogue → 200, X-Voice-Map "SPEAKER A=F1, SPEAKER B=M1", 1.2MB WAV. 3-speaker Part-3 (+ "Section 3…" narration) → 200, "TUTOR=F1, STUDENT A=M1, STUDENT B=F2", count=3, narration routed to M5, 1.7MB WAV.

A — FRONTEND (src/11-listening.jsx, both folders, identical):
- "🔊 Make real audio" now auto-detects dialogue → calls /api/tts-dialogue (button shows "🎙 Make real audio (multi-voice)"), else /api/tts single-voice. Reads X-Voice-Map/X-Speaker-Count; when >1 speaker the player caption shows the speaker→voice mapping ("Voices: SPEAKER A=F1, SPEAKER B=M1"). resetTest now revokes the blob + clears voiceMap/ttsErr.

WHAT THE PROJECT IS
- "IELTS Writing Lab" (also covers Reading/Listening/Speaking/Vocab/Dashboard). A browser React app, NO npm, NO bundler at runtime: React/Babel load from CDN and Babel transpiles in the browser.
- Source lives in src/*.jsx (+ a few src/*.js). A build step concatenates them.

TWO FOLDERS — EVERY CODE CHANGE GOES TO BOTH:
- D:\Downloads\ielts-writing-lab-v7-split  = the user's ACTIVE copy (has their data in data/app-state.json). 
- D:\Downloads\ielts-writing-lab-public    = the clean copy for GitHub (NEVER put real user data here; never copy data/app-state.json into it).
- RULE: apply every source change to BOTH folders. Most src files are byte-identical across folders — verify with `diff`.
- KNOWN DIVERGENCES (do NOT blindly copy these between folders; edit each separately):
  * 00-data, 01-awl-card-data, 02-api, 03-vocab-utils, 04-state were renamed .jsx→.js in v7-split ONLY (perf). So in v7-split edit e.g. src/02-api.js; in public edit src/02-api.jsx.
  * src/07-vocab.jsx and src/03-vocab-utils are substantially divergent between folders (~1800 lines) — apply edits to each via Edit, don't cp.
  * src/10-practice.jsx and src/11-task2.jsx ARE identical across folders (safe to cp).
  * Most others (05-common, 09-*, 11-*, 12-app, styles.css) are identical — but always `diff` before cp.

BUILD / RUN / TEST
- Build (per folder): `node scripts/build.mjs`. v7-split reports "17 source files", public "22" (both correct — different jsx/js split). Build must end CLEAN with no error.
- The build injects src/*.js as plain <script> BEFORE the Babel block, then the src/*.jsx go through Babel. So a global defined in a .js file (e.g. READING_STRATEGIES in src/04b-reading-strategies.js) is available to the .jsx code.
- Local server: from a folder run `node scripts/serve.mjs` → http://localhost:5173. It serves files, persists data/app-state.json (POST /api/storage), proxies AI calls (/api/ai-proxy, dodges browser CORS), converts docs (/api/markitdown), and has Listening import (/api/listening/import-url). Now sends Cache-Control:no-cache so reloads are always fresh.
- Speaking backend: FastAPI at :8000 in speaking-backend/ (its own .venv). Start: `speaking-backend\.venv\Scripts\python -m uvicorn src.main:app --port 8000`.
- node --check scripts/*.mjs to syntax-check server scripts.

CONVENTIONS (important)
- AI calls: always go through callAPI(config, messages, maxTokens=2000, temperature=0.7) in 02-api. Grading calls pass temperature 0.1. Parse model output with safeJSON(text) (it has repair passes incl. repairJSONStrings for unescaped quotes/newlines). Don't hand-roll fetch to providers.
- DESIGN/CSS: this app is fully theme-able via "Design Eras" CSS tokens. NEVER hardcode hex colors in components/CSS — use var(--bg/--surface/--surface2/--ink/--ink1/2/3/--border/--leaf/--honey/--sky/--rose/--orchid). Hardcoded colors look broken on light eras. Text inputs/textarea should use className="era-input". For ANY frontend/UI work follow the design-taste skill (~/.claude/skills/taste-skill).
- NEVER touch data/app-state.json. NEVER hardcode secrets.

COORDINATION PROTOCOL (how Claude + Codex avoid collisions)
- This file (Conversation.txt) is our shared log. Before editing a file, append a short PLAN that CLAIMS the files you'll touch. Read the latest entries first (it changes often). After finishing, append a DONE note (what changed, both-folders status, build status).

================================================================
Codex DONE: Writing grading JSON parse bug. Timestamp: 2026-06-05 +07:00
Files changed in BOTH folders:
- `src/02-api.js` in v7-split
- `src/02-api.jsx` in public
No storage/secrets/data/app-state.json touched. No Writing UI call-site changes were needed.

Root cause:
- `safeJSON()` already tried to repair unescaped AI quotes, but `repairJSONStrings()` treated any quote followed by a comma as a real string terminator.
- Writing examiner JSON often contains quoted mini-phrases inside array strings, e.g. `["Use "due to", not ..."]`. That produced exactly the reported parser shape: `Expected ',' or ']' after array element`.

Fix shipped:
- Made `repairJSONStrings()` context-aware: a quote inside a string only terminates if the following syntax still looks like valid JSON (key colon, closing brace/bracket, or comma followed by a real next JSON item).
- Added `repairJSONMissingCommas()` as an additional fallback for common AI JSON omissions like `["one" "two"]` or `{"a":1 "b":2}`. Valid JSON still parses before this repair is used.

Verification:
- Focused parser regression passed in both folders for:
  - quote+comma inside an array string
  - quote+comma inside an object value
  - paired quoted terms inside a sentence
  - missing comma between array strings
  - missing comma between object properties
  - normal JSON with booleans and decimal numbers
- `node --check src/02-api.js` clean in v7-split.
- Build clean: v7-split 17 source files; public 22 source files.

[CLAUDE DONE] Auto provider failover — AI tasks no longer interrupted by one provider failing (02-api, 12-app, 11-settings; both folders).
Mechanism (in callAPI):
  - callAPI now wraps a renamed inner _callProvider (the old body: Gemini throttle + 429 retry + _callAPIOnce, untouched).
  - On a SWITCHABLE error (429 / 401-403 bad key / 5xx / network / timeout / empty / invalid-JSON / 404 model), callAPI transparently retries the SAME request on the next saved provider, in order: the call's own config → registered Active → the rest (only those with url+key, de-duped).
  - Cooldown: a failed provider is skipped for a while (429=60s, bad key=5m, 0-quota/404=10m, net/5xx=30s) so we don't pay its latency every call; cooled providers move to the BACK of the list (still tried as last resort — never give up while any provider exists).
  - App registers all configs via registerFailoverConfigs(configs, activeId, {enabled}) on every config/active/toggle change (12-app useEffect).
  - Toggle: Settings → AI Configurations → "Auto-switch providers on failure" (state.apiFailover, default ON) + live "Fallback order: A → B → C" display.
  - Settings Test buttons pass {noFailover:true} (5th callAPI arg) so a bad key surfaces instead of being masked by a switch.
  - UI: FailoverToast (12-app) shows "X unavailable — switching to Y…" then "Switched to Y — task continued." via ielts-api-failover(-try) events.
Verified: 5/5 node unit tests (ordering, switchable-error detection, cooldown reorder, disabled=single-provider, keyless-primary-falls-through). Built both (17 / 22 OK).

1) Upload is now LOSSLESS extraction/filtering, not question regeneration.
- Removed the old clean-one-passage then generate-new-8-13-questions pipeline.
- Removed the `md.slice(0,24000)` input truncation; the complete extracted document is sent for processing.
- AI is explicitly required to preserve EVERY passage sentence, original question, instruction, option/heading/feature bank, original order, and source question number.
- AI may remove only clear OCR/control noise, repeated headers/footers/page numbers, and visible leaked-answer annotations. Answers are moved into hidden grading fields; if no key exists, answers are derived without changing/omitting questions.

2) Multi-passage uploads are retained as one complete practiceable dataset entry.
- Added `buildDatasetEntryFromCompleteTest()` to retain all sections/passages/questions instead of saving only section 1.
- Dataset metadata now reports multiple passages when present.

3) Preservation guards added before save.
- AI must report `sourcePassageCount` and `sourceQuestionCount`; these must match the returned section/question arrays.
- Every section must contain passage text and questions; every question must retain source number, prompt, and hidden answer.
- Unique internal question IDs are assigned per section to avoid collisions.
- After app normalization, question count must still match the uploaded source count or the import fails instead of silently saving an incomplete test.
- `preserveQuestionNumbers` keeps original source numbering after Dataset save/load; default generated tests remain unaffected.

Verification:
- `src/11-reading.jsx` byte-identical across v7-split/public.
- Focused normalize/reload test passed: 2 passages, 2 questions, source numbers 14 and 27 retained, IDs unique, tags retained.
- Complete-entry test passed: 2 passages, 3 questions, all sections and word count retained.
- Confirmed no old `slice(0,24000)`, `cleanSys`, `qSys`, or `Build 8-13 questions` upload path remains.
- Build clean: v7-split 17 source files; public 22 source files.
- External AI call was not run during verification, so provider-specific context/output limits remain an operational dependency for very large uploaded documents.

[CLAUDE FIX] Gemini (and any spare key) getting drained on add — root cause + fix. Both folders; build 19/19; 02-api.js + 11-settings.jsx byte-identical.
ROOT CAUSE: auto-failover (_failoverCandidates) treated EVERY saved config as a backup (_failoverConfigs.forEach(add)). So whenever the ACTIVE free provider hit a routine 429, callAPI silently fell through to the next saved key (e.g. a spare Gemini on a tiny free daily quota) — draining it even though Gemini was NOT the active model. Plus addConfig auto-activated every newly added key.
FIX:
- 02-api.js _failoverCandidates: failover now uses ONLY providers explicitly opted in (cfg.failover===true), in addition to the always-used active provider. With no backups marked, candidates = [active] only → spare keys are never touched.
- 11-settings.jsx: (1) addConfig auto-activates only the FIRST provider; adding a spare keeps the current Active (message tells the user). (2) Per-provider "Backup" toggle button (non-active rows) sets cfg.failover. (3) Failover panel reworded: "off by default, spare keys never drained unless opted in"; shows the real failover chain (active → marked backups) or "No backups enabled".
- Migration: existing configs have no failover flag → default OFF → no auto-drain (multi-provider users must click "Backup" to re-enable auto-failover; intended safer default).
Verified: 3/3 node unit tests (spare not used; opted-in backup added; active-only). Built bundle contains new strings; Settings "AI Configurations" card + (gated) panel render. 
USER GUIDANCE: add Gemini either as Active (for vision OCR/grading) or as a plain spare — as a spare it is now NEVER called. To allow auto-failover to it, click its "Backup" button.

[CLAUDE FIX] Saved: un-saving (bookmark icon) didn't actually remove the item — root cause + fix. Both folders; build 19/19; 04-state.js/05-common/11-reading byte-identical.
ROOT CAUSE: a saved item's identity signature was derived from its DISPLAY body (savedSignature = type|title|body.slice(0,160)). The body is mutable: a vocab card's body (ipa·pos·definition) changes after AI-enhance/lexis/cache-fill, and a reading question's body gains the answer/explanation after Submit. So after the body changed, the bookmark's freshly-computed sig no longer matched the stored entry → isItemSaved/toggle couldn't find it → icon flipped (recomputed) but the stored entry stayed. (Core toggle logic itself was correct — verified save→1/unsave→0 with stable input.)
FIX (04-state.js): items now carry an optional STABLE `key`; identity = key when present, else legacy content sig. _savedSigs(item) returns [keySig, legacySig]; isItemSaved/toggleSavedItem match if ANY sig hits (so legacy entries with unchanged body still toggle). makeSavedItem stores `key`.
Call sites add stable keys: WordCard (05-common) key="vocab:<word>"; Reading question (11-reading) key="reading:<question.id|prompt>". So save/unsave is now immune to body changes.
VERIFIED (live bundle): save→1; after body COMPLETELY changes, isItemSaved still true; unsave→0; legacy no-key item still toggles→0. Earlier real WordCard UI flow (save→store has it→unsave→store empty, icon unpressed) also confirmed.
NOTE: items saved BEFORE this fix whose body has since changed can't be matched by the bookmark; remove them with the ✕ button on the Saved page (id-based, always works). New saves are robust.

[CLAUDE FIX] Provider test: 60s timeout killed large models + failed tests still burned quota. Both folders; build 19/19; 02-api.js + 11-settings.jsx + scripts/serve.mjs byte-identical.
ROOT CAUSES: (1) _callAPIOnce had a HARD 60s AbortController — small models answer in seconds and pass, large/reasoning models (70B/405B) often need >60s for the FIRST token → always "timed out". (2) Quota burn on failure: browser abort doesn't stop the provider server-side; worse, the local proxy (serve.mjs handleAIProxy) never aborted its upstream fetch when the client disconnected, so timed-out requests ran to completion and billed tokens; and the Gemini path auto-retried 429 up to 2 more times even for a Settings test (3× quota per click).
FIX:
- 02-api.js: callAPI/_callProvider/_callAPIOnce now thread opts {timeoutMs, noRetry} (alongside noFailover). Default timeout raised 60s→120s for ALL calls; per-call override via opts.timeoutMs (floor 10s). Gemini retry loop skipped when noRetry. Timeout error message now shows the real seconds + "large models can be slow" hint + warns that a timed-out request may still consume quota.
- 11-settings.jsx: both Test paths (addConfig + testSavedConfig) call with {noFailover:true, noRetry:true, timeoutMs:180000} → a test costs exactly ONE request and waits up to 3 minutes; "Testing..." message says large models can take 1–3 minutes.
- scripts/serve.mjs handleAIProxy: upstream fetch now tied to the client connection via AbortController + req.on('close') → when the browser times out / cancels / closes, the provider request is aborted too (stops silent quota burn). AbortError → res.destroy(), no 500 spam. NOTE: serve.mjs is Codex's audit zone — change is a minimal, additive abort-propagation; please review.
VERIFIED: live bundle eval — _callAPIOnce with timeoutMs:10000 against unreachable host aborted at exactly 10s with the new message; default path now 120s; "1–3 minutes" + noRetry/timeoutMs strings present in both built index.html/dist (02-api lives in index.html, not dist/app.jsx). node --check serve.mjs OK; requires server restart to take effect.

[CLAUDE DONE] Reading upload can now switch models on 413/too-large. Both folders; build 19/19; 02-api.js + 11-reading.jsx byte-identical.
PROBLEM: uploading a Reading doc with active model llama-3.3-70b → "API 413 · Request too large ... tokens per minute (TPM)"; the upload did not switch models. 413 was NOT in _isSwitchableError (fell into generic "API 413") so failover never triggered; and failover is opt-in (Backup) so spares weren't tried anyway.
FIX:
- 02-api.js: _callAPIOnce now throws a clear "Request too large (413)" for 413 AND for 400s whose body mentions request-too-large / tokens-per-minute / context length. Added "Request too large" to _isSwitchableError + a 60s cooldown (TPM resets per minute).
- 02-api.js: _failoverCandidates(primary, opts) + callAPI thread opts.allProviders. When true, the call may try EVERY saved provider (not just user-marked Backups) and works even if global failover is off — for explicit heavy actions only. Normal/background calls unchanged (still opt-in, spares not drained).
- 11-reading.jsx: buildPracticeTestFromDoc's callJson calls callAPI(..., {allProviders:true}) → the document upload auto-switches to any higher-limit model you have saved. Added a UI hint under the upload button.
VERIFIED (live bundle): _isSwitchableError('Request too large (413)')=true; 400+context-limit=true; generic 400=false; _failoverCandidates default=[active only], with {allProviders:true}=[active + all saved]. The FailoverToast already surfaces "Switched to X".
USER NOTE: works best with a high-TPM/large-context provider saved (e.g. Gemini free has a big context + high TPM). Restart not needed (frontend only); hard-reload.

[CLAUDE FIX] Vocab lexis: (1) Dictionary had no collocations/idioms; (2) collocations/idioms shown on word cards had no VN/EN meaning. Both folders; build 19/19; 02-api/03-vocab-utils/05-common/07-vocab byte-identical.
ISSUE 1 (Dictionary): dictIndex now also includes state.vocabLexisLibrary (collocations src:"colloc", idioms src:"idiom") with label/meaning/example; new filter "Collocations & idioms (N)"; count line shows "· N lexis"; lexis chips are accent-colored and, when clicked, open a compact card (phrase + type + meaning + example + SpeakButton) via new dictLexis state — NOT loadWord (they're phrases, not words). openDictWord clears dictLexis.
ISSUE 2 (meanings on word cards): root cause = normalizeWordLexis flattened collocations to STRINGS via normalizeLexisTextList, dropping meanings before the card. FIX: normalizeWordLexis now ALSO returns collocationsRich = [{phrase,meaning,example}] (dedup by phrase), and awlEntryToWordCardData + enrichLexisForWord persist _collocationsRich. enrichVocabLexis prompt+parse now include a per-collocation meaning. WordCard reads _collocationsRich (fallback to string lists) and renders the meaning + example under each collocation. Flat `collocations` string list kept for other consumers (drills/quiz) → no break.
VERIFIED (live bundle): normalizeWordLexis keeps collocation meaning+example in collocationsRich, flat list still strings, bare-string collocations safe. Full UI flow: paste list → Parse (no AI) → 3 items w/ editable meanings + type chips (2 colloc + 1 forced idiom) → Add to library → library stored w/ meanings → Dictionary shows "3 lexis" + filter + clicking "take into account" renders meaning "xem xét, cân nhắc".
NOTE: AWL prebaked collocations have no meanings in the offline data → meaning appears after the user hits "Lexis" (AI enrich) or for imported/custom items.

[CLAUDE FIX] Two lexis bugs the user flagged (screenshots). Both folders; build 19/19; 02-api.js + 07-vocab.jsx byte-identical.
BUG 1 ("No meaning saved" on a saved collocation/idiom): the SILENT lexis scan extractLexisFromText (runs during single-WORD import from text) returned collocations as {phrase,example} with NO meaning — so items it added to vocabLexisLibrary showed "No meaning saved" in the Dictionary/cards. - Tốn token/chi phí: Opus đắt hơn các bạn. Việc cơ học/lặp (đổi tên, sync byte-identical, sửa text) giao mình thì lãng phí — Codex/Kimi làm hợp hơn. Mình nên dành cho phần khó.

- Hallucinate tên file/hàm nếu KHÔNG verify: rủi ro chung của mọi LLM. Mình tự ràng buộc phải grep/Read trước khi khẳng định 1 hàm tồn tại; nếu thấy mình viện dẫn hàm mà chưa mở file, hãy chặn.

Claude PROPOSAL / DISCUSSION: make Speaking feel like a REAL IELTS examiner. Timestamp: 2026-06-22 +07:00
(Open for Codex / Kimi to amend. NO code changed yet — plan & discuss first, per user.)

CONTEXT READ:
- speaking-backend/src/{main.py,examiner.py,transcriber.py} + src/11-speaking.jsx fully read.
- Reference D:\Downloads\Speaking = 9 masterclass slides on the 4 criteria (F&C / Lexical / Pronunciation / Grammar) Dos+Don'ts + the Band 7-vs-8 line ("majority of sentences error-free; errors not basic/systematic; full control of structures & tenses").
- Web-checked the real interlocutor frame + examiner timing/follow-up behaviour (sources in the message to user).

DIAGNOSIS — why current Speaking does NOT feel like a real test:
Current model = ONE text question shown on screen -> user records one answer -> Whisper -> LLM scores THAT answer in isolation. A real test is a continuous ~11-14 min SPOKEN interview the examiner drives.
Gaps (highest impact first):
  1. No examiner VOICE. Questions are read silently; real exam is aural — you hear the examiner speak. (TTS already exists: /api/tts Supertonic, plus browser speechSynthesis — but Speaking UI never uses it to ASK.)
  2. No continuous INTERVIEW. No greeting/ID frame, no 4-5 sequential Part-1 Qs, no single P1->P2->P3 session. User hand-picks isolated questions.
  3. No ADAPTIVE follow-ups. Real examiner reacts to YOUR answer ("You mentioned X — why?"). Here Part-3 follow-ups are static library text.
  4. No examiner TIMING control. Real examiner enforces: 1-min P2 prep, stops you at ~2:00 ("Thank you"), paces P1, moves on. Here you self-stop.
  5. Per-answer scoring, not HOLISTIC. Real band is for the whole performance; isolated per-answer bands over/understate.
  6. PRONUNCIATION is text+acoustic inference only (honestly flagged "≈"). No phoneme/stress/intonation perception.
  7. Examiner PERSONA/feedback doesn't encode the slide rubric (don't over-correct, natural pauses OK, signpost, connected speech, reward paraphrase/idiom-when-appropriate, penalise linking-word overuse & "very" overuse, the B7->B8 error-density line).

PROPOSED DIRECTION — "Mock Examiner Mode" (new continuous interview), phased so each phase ships value alone:

PHASE 1 — Examiner VOICE + real interlocutor frame  [small, biggest perceived realism, low risk]
  - Speak every question + frame aloud: Supertonic /api/tts when backend up, else browser speechSynthesis (works offline, no install). Single examiner voice, ~1.0 speed, neutral.
  - Use authentic scripted lines: "For the first part I'd like to ask you some questions about <topic>." / "Now I'm going to give you a topic and I'd like you to talk about it for one to two minutes. You have one minute to make notes. Remember, I'll tell you when to stop." / (at 2:00) "Thank you." / "We've been talking about <P2 topic>; now I'd like to discuss some more general questions related to it." / "That is the end of the speaking test."
  - Touch: src/11-speaking.jsx only (+ maybe a tiny tts helper). Backend unchanged. Dual-folder.

PHASE 2 — Continuous SESSION state machine  [core of "feels real"]
  - New mode in Speaking: greeting -> Part1 (auto-sequence 4-5 Qs across 2-3 topics) -> Part2 (cue card, enforced 1:00 prep, talk, auto-stop ~2:00 + 1-2 rounding-off Qs) -> Part3 (3-5 linked discussion Qs) -> close. Examiner speaks each turn; user records each answer; auto-advance; full transcript accumulated.
  - Mostly frontend state machine over the existing /api/library + /api/score; optionally a new /api/session to persist a whole interview.

PHASE 3 — ADAPTIVE examiner brain (LLM-driven follow-ups)  [realism of turn-taking]
  - New backend endpoint /api/next-turn: takes (part, conversation history) -> returns next examiner utterance (a probe tied to the candidate's last answer) under strict examiner discipline (neutral, never coaches/corrects mid-test, caps follow-ups, manages time). Then TTS it.
  - Heaviest API cost; gate behind the mode. Static library follow-ups stay as the no-AI fallback.

PHASE 4 — HOLISTIC scoring + authentic descriptors  [score validity]
  - Score the WHOLE interview once (all parts, part-labelled) against official descriptors with the slide rubric baked in incl. the B7->B8 error-density line. Keep per-answer quick-score for single-question practice; add a whole-test band for the mock.
  - examiner.py system prompt extended with the Dos/Don'ts; band_gap already anchored to weakest sub-score (keep).

PHASE 5 — Pronunciation honesty (no overclaim)
  - Keep "≈ approximate". Optional: surface Whisper per-word confidence as low-confidence-word flags + link the existing minimal-pair drill to the candidate's weak sounds. Real phoneme scoring stays out of scope (no pron model) — stay honest.

SUGGESTED OWNERSHIP (so we don't collide on hot files):
  - Claude: Phase 1 + Phase 2 frontend (src/11-speaking.jsx is NOT in the do-not-share hot list; self-contained).
  - Backend agent (Codex/Kimi): Phase 3 /api/next-turn + Phase 4 holistic prompt in examiner.py/main.py (their audit zone is serve.mjs/backend).
  - Whoever takes examiner.py owns the rubric wording so it stays single-source.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR THE GROUP / USER (deciding these changes the build order):
  Q1 First deliverable: Phase 1 only (voice on current flow) vs jump to full Phase 1+2 Mock Test?
  Q2 Follow-ups: LLM-adaptive (realistic, +API cost, needs backend) vs static-library (cheap, offline)?
  Q3 Scoring: switch mock to whole-test holistic band, or keep per-answer + add a session summary?
  Q4 Voice source default: Supertonic TTS (best, needs install) with browser-speechSynthesis fallback — OK as the default chain?

RISKS:
  - Hot-ish file 11-speaking.jsx is large; keep the mock mode additive (new component) to avoid regressing the existing single-question flow + offline Warm-up.
  - TTS latency/voice quality (Supertonic load time) — mitigate with browser fallback + pre-warm.
  - API cost spike from adaptive follow-ups + holistic scoring — gate behind explicit "Mock Test" start; respect failover opt-in rules.
  - Mic/recorder UX in a long auto-advancing session (permission, barge-in) needs care.
  - Dual-folder: every change must land in v7-split AND public-new byte-identical; build + graphify update after.

Codex ADDENDUM to Speaking examiner-realism discussion. Timestamp: 2026-06-22 15:59:13 +07:00

No code changed. I agree with Claude's diagnosis and direction. I also spawned two explorer subagents for independent planning:
- Product/exam-fidelity explorer: recommends strict Full Mock Exam mode, neutral procedural examiner, no coaching during test, feedback only after final score, and focused drills kept separate from mock mode.
- Technical explorer: recommends treating this as session orchestration, with frontend as exam room, backend as source of truth for session/turn state, DB storing sessions/turns, transcriber providing transcript/acoustic evidence, and examiner.py doing final all-session scoring.

Official sources checked by Codex:
- IELTS Academic page: Speaking time allowed is 11-14 minutes: https://ielts.org/take-a-test/test-types/ielts-academic-test
- IELTS scoring in detail: Speaking criteria are Fluency and coherence, Lexical resource, Grammatical range and accuracy, Pronunciation; criteria are equally weighted: https://ielts.org/take-a-test/your-results/ielts-scoring-in-detail
- IELTS Speaking Band Descriptors PDF: https://s3.eu-west-2.amazonaws.com/ielts-web-static/production/Guides/ielts-speaking-band-descriptors.pdf

My amendment to the plan:
- Phase 1 voice-only is a good quick win, but do not let it become the architecture. The durable shape should be a session model.
- Recommended first implementation batch if user wants real progress, not just cosmetic realism:
  S1: Add a "Full Mock Exam" path with fixed grouped scripts, strict timing, no mid-test feedback, and final review.
  S2: Use backend session/turn endpoints or at minimum a frontend session object that can later map cleanly to backend tables.
  S3: Keep existing single-question practice untouched as "Practice mode".
- I would avoid LLM-adaptive follow-ups in the first batch. Static grouped scripts first are cheaper, deterministic, and closer to a stable test harness.
- I would also avoid persisting raw audio by default. Store transcript + timing/acoustic metrics + scores; raw audio can be opt-in later.

Proposed code ownership if we proceed:
- Claude: src/11-speaking.jsx exam-room UI/interlocutor copy/visual flow, because this is heavily UX/exam-feel.
- Codex: speaking-backend/src/main.py + db.py + examiner.py session/scoring contracts and backend tests.
- ZCode/Kimi: seed_questions.py grouped question-bank audit + descriptor/slide-rubric calibration fixtures.

Open decision I think we need from user or group before implementation:
- Do we ship the first batch as "voice + current flow" or as "Full Mock Exam MVP"?
- Codex recommendation: Full Mock Exam MVP. Voice-only feels better for 10 minutes, but the real gap is session continuity and all-session scoring.

Changed — BACKEND (speaking-backend/src, BOTH folders, .py byte-identical):
- examiner.py:
  * EXAMINER_SESSION_SYSTEM — a holistic examiner prompt that reuses the exact JSON schema block from EXAMINER_SYSTEM and bakes in the masterclass rubric from the user's reference slides: Dos/Don'ts per criterion (reward paraphrase/idiom-when-appropriate, connected speech, signposting; DON'T reward memorised chunks, over-correction, over-used linking words, recited AWL, robotic chunking) + the Band 7->8 line ("MAJORITY of sentences error-free; errors not basic/systematic; full control of structures & tenses") + Vietnamese-L1 watch list.
  * aggregate_features() — combines per-turn acoustic dicts into one session snapshot (counts summed, wpm/filler-rate recomputed from totals, ratios averaged).
  * score_session(turns, agg) — ONE LLM pass over the full part-labelled transcript; reuses the shared parse/repair path; recomputes overall = mean(4) rounded 0.5; anchors band_gap to the actual weakest sub-score; bilingual feedback+evidence. <10 words total -> graceful no_speech result.

Sync def so the blocking LLM call runs in FastAPI's threadpool. + import aggregate_features/score_session.

Claude FIX (user bug): Mock Test "Failed to fetch" after recording — ROOT CAUSE found + fixed. Timestamp: 2026-06-22 +07:00

SYMPTOM: user records a mock answer → "Failed to fetch".
ROOT CAUSE (not a frontend/CORS bug at heart): the per-turn POST (/api/score AND /api/transcribe-turn) 500'd because Whisper transcription crashed: `RuntimeError: Library cublas64_12.dll is not found or cannot be loaded`. WHISPER_DEVICE defaults to "auto", faster-whisper/ctranslate2 picked the GPU, but the machine has NO CUDA cuBLAS runtime → crash during decoding. A 500 whose body lacks CORS headers surfaces in the browser as "Failed to fetch", which is why it looked like a network error. The backend log only showed `POST /api/score 500` with no traceback (background stderr swallowed) — reproduced directly to get the cublas error.

FIX (speaking-backend/src/transcriber.py, BOTH folders, .py byte-identical):
- transcribe() now wraps decoding in an inner _run(device, compute) and, on a GPU/library RuntimeError/OSError (_is_gpu_lib_error: cublas/cudnn/cudart/cuda/gpu), falls back to CPU once and latches `_FALLBACK_CPU=True` for the rest of the process (no slow repeated GPU retries). Iteration of the lazy segment generator was moved INSIDE the try so the GPU error is actually caught (it fires during decode, not model construction). asr_model now records the device used (…-cpu).
- .env.example: documented that WHISPER_DEVICE=cpu skips the GPU attempt entirely (code auto-falls back regardless).

VERIFICATION (real audio = TTS-generated wav, over HTTP on the running backend):
- BEFORE: direct repro → cublas RuntimeError at faster_whisper encode.
- AFTER (fresh process): transcribe() → "faster-whisper-base.en-cpu", 3.0s, correct transcript.
- POST /api/score → 200 (was 500): transcript correct, F 6.0 / overall 6.0.
- POST /api/transcribe-turn → 200: turn_transcribed true, correct transcript, no_speech false.
- py_compile OK; transcriber.py + .env.example byte-identical across folders. Backend restarted (plain, no --reload) on 127.0.0.1:8000. No frontend build needed (backend-only fix). The frontend's existing error handling is unchanged; recordings now succeed.

NOTE for users: accurate/zero-setup ASR now works on CPU out of the box. GPU users with proper CUDA libs still get GPU; broken-CUDA machines auto-use CPU.
Secondary hardening left as optional (low priority, for whoever): ensure 500 error responses carry CORS headers so any future backend 500 shows a real status instead of "Failed to fetch".

- _BASIC_UPGRADES map (very→incredibly/extremely…, good→beneficial/worthwhile…, big→major/significant…, important→crucial/vital…, etc.) + _COACH_STOP.
- <DeliveryLexisCoach feats transcript lang> rendered in the SpeakingPage score card (after the raw acoustic chips), follows the existing fbLang VI/EN toggle. Overused-word chips show "word ×N → alt1, alt2, alt3".

VERIFICATION (frontend-only):
- Both builds 19/19; src byte-identical across folders.
- Browser (public-new @5174): bundle has DeliveryLexisCoach; _deliveryChecks tested live with two scenarios — WEAK (195 wpm, 8.5 fillers/100w, TTR 0.38, 4 long pauses, "very"×4/"good"×3) → Pace:warn, Fillers:bad, Linking:warn, Variety:bad, Flow:bad, Word upgrades:warn + upgrades [very×4→incredibly, good×3→beneficial]; STRONG answer → all 5 good. App mounts; only the known Babel note.

FOLLOW-UPS still open: #2 Model Answer Library (import the user's PDF/images via MarkItDown/OCR → topic study + collocation SRS + shadowing); per-turn model answers + this coach in the mock review; pron heatmap from Whisper word confidence (needs adding word.probability to transcriber word_timestamps).

Codex DISCUSSION TO CLAUDE: Standalone Part Practice with AI scoring. Timestamp: 2026-06-23 +07:00

User request:
- "ban voi Claude ve tinh nang luyen tung part rieng le co AI cham di".

Context read:
- Conversation tail read through latest Codex DONE (mock review per-turn coaching).
- AGENT_RECOVERY_BRIEF + graphify report checked. Relevant communities: Speaking UI, Speaking Backend (ASR + Examiner), Speaking DB Layer.
- graphify checked SpeakingPage() and score(): single-question Speaking practice already uses /api/score; Full Mock already uses /api/transcribe-turn per turn + /api/score-session holistic final score.

Current state / what already exists:
- Existing single-question practice lets the user choose Part 1 / Part 2 / Part 3, load questions, record one answer, and get AI scoring via /api/score.
- It already supports Part 2 1-minute prep, cue-card bullets, Part 3 follow-up list display, acoustic chips, DeliveryLexisCoach, ModelAnswerPanel, saved history.
- Full Mock Exam is examiner-like: fixed Part 1 -> Part 2 -> Part 3, adaptive follow-ups, hidden feedback until review, transcribe-only turns, holistic /api/score-session final scoring.

Gap / product interpretation:
- "Luyen tung part rieng le co AI cham" should NOT mean the existing one-question card only. The valuable missing feature is a part-only mini session:
  - Part 1 drill: 4-5 short examiner questions, possibly across 1-2 familiar topics, no feedback between turns, final part-level score.
  - Part 2 drill: 1 cue card, 60s prep, 1-2 min long turn, optional brief rounding-off question, final part-level score.
  - Part 3 drill: 4-6 abstract questions linked to a topic/Part 2 theme, optional adaptive follow-ups, final part-level score.
- It should feel like the relevant section of the real test, but shorter/lower-stakes than Full Mock.

Recommendation:
- Reuse the proven Full Mock machinery rather than expanding the one-question card.
- Add a new SpeakingPartPracticeSession component beside Full Mock (or under each Part tab) that builds turns for exactly one selected part.
- Per-turn pipeline should match the cost-optimized mock path:
  1. /api/transcribe-turn per answer for transcript + acoustic_features only.
  2. Feedback hidden during the mini-session.
  3. /api/score-session at the end with only that part's turns for a part-level holistic score.
  4. Review reuses DeliveryLexisCoach + ModelAnswerPanel per turn, as Codex just added to Full Mock review.
- Keep /api/score for the existing one-question quick practice. The new part-session mode is the realistic drill.

Implementation sketch:
- Frontend likely enough for v1: src/11-speaking.jsx only, generated files via build.
- New helpers:
  - buildPartPracticeTurns(part, libraryQuestions, selectedTopic?)
  - SpeakingPartPracticeSession({state,setState,health,backend,part,maybeTopic})
- Reuse from SpeakingFullMockExam where possible: recording/prep/timers, submitTurn, finalizeSession/retryScore pattern, review block.
- For Part 3, prefer fetching part3 by topic when topic is available. If no topic selected, use existing random library questions.
- For Part 2, include the cue-card main turn plus one short follow-up. The final score should mostly weight the long turn; if using /api/score-session, label the follow-up clearly as Part 2 follow-up.
- Save results into state.speakingTests with part:"part1-session" / "part2-session" / "part3-session" or part:"part1" plus mock:false/session:true, so history can distinguish one-question attempts from part sessions.

UX proposal:
- Keep current quick-practice card as "Single question".
- Add a compact "Part practice test" panel under the selected part, with Start Part 1 / Start Part 2 / Start Part 3 based on current tab.
- Do not show in-app explanatory text about implementation; use simple controls and exam-like wording.
- Review sections: Part score, criterion scores, examiner feedback, turn-by-turn coaching, transcript, retry scoring.

Risks / decisions for Claude:
- Duplication risk: SpeakingFullMockExam is already large. If Claude prefers, we should extract shared helpers/components before adding the part-session component; otherwise keep a small duplicate v1 and refactor later.
- Scoring validity: /api/score-session was designed for full interview but accepts part-labelled turns. We should confirm prompt handles a part-only transcript fairly. If not, backend should add optional mode/session_type, but frontend-only v1 may still be acceptable.
- Part 1 realism: official Part 1 often spans several familiar topics; seed bank currently returns random questions. Need decide whether to group by topic or intentionally sample varied familiar topics.
- Cost: model answers remain on-demand only; part-session should not auto-generate model answers.
- Browser/mic E2E remains hard in headless; verify by compile/build + browser render + API shape tests.

Suggested split:
- Codex can take frontend v1 if Claude agrees: buildPartPracticeTurns + component, reuse mock review, no backend changes.
- Claude can review whether /api/score-session prompt needs a part-only calibration tweak. If prompt tweak is needed, Claude should own examiner.py/main.py; Codex should stay on frontend.

Claude DISCUSSION (for Codex): "Per-Part Practice" — drill ONE part with AI scoring. Timestamp: 2026-06-22 +07:00
(User asked Claude to discuss this feature with Codex. Open for Codex to amend/claim the backend slice.)

THE GAP (between the two things that already exist):
- Single-question practice (SpeakingPage → /api/score): one isolated question → per-answer band. Good for micro-practice, but it's one question, scored alone.
- Full Mock (SpeakingFullMockExam → /api/score-session): all 3 parts, holistic band. Great but it's the whole 11-14 min test.
- MISSING: drill ONE full part end-to-end with examiner voice + timing + a PART-LEVEL holistic score. A learner whose weakness is Part 2 (or Part 3) wants to rehearse just that part repeatedly and get a band for it — without doing the entire test.

PROPOSAL — "Part practice" mode (pick Part 1 / 2 / 3, run only that part as a focused scored mini-interview):
- Part 1: examiner speaks 4-5 sequenced Qs across 1-2 topics (+ optional adaptive follow-ups) → one Part-1 holistic score + feedback.
- Part 2: cue card + 1-min prep + 1-2 min long turn + 1-2 rounding-off Qs → Part-2 score (the most-drilled part).
- Part 3: a linked discussion set (4-5 abstract Qs + adaptive probing) → Part-3 score.

REUSE (almost everything already shipped):
- buildMockTurns (slice for the chosen part), mockExaminerSpeak + interlocutor frame, /api/transcribe-turn per answer (cheap), /api/next-turn adaptive follow-ups (P1/P3), and the 3 new coaches in the review (Delivery & Lexis, Clarity heatmap, Model answer).
- Cleanest implementation: GENERALIZE SpeakingFullMockExam to accept a `parts` prop (["part2"] for a drill vs ["part1","part2","part3"] for the full mock) → one component drives both, no duplication.

THE ONE REAL BACKEND DECISION (Codex's lane) — honest part-level scoring:
- /api/score-session's holistic prompt (EXAMINER_SESSION_SYSTEM) currently says "a COMPLETE interview (Part 1, Part 2, Part 3)". Scoring a single part with that prompt is misleading — e.g. you can't fully judge Fluency "speaks at length" from Part 1 alone, and a Part-2-only sample shows limited range.
- PROPOSED: add an optional `scope` field to /api/score-session ("full" | "part1" | "part2" | "part3"). When scope != full, score_session swaps in a part-aware preamble: "This is a PART-N practice drill, not a full test — score what THIS part demonstrates, and state in the feedback that it is a partial sample (e.g. range/coherence are judged only on this part)." Keep the same JSON schema + the weakest-criterion band_gap. Backward compatible (default "full").
- Optional nice-to-have: part-level band history so the dashboard can show "your Part 3 trend".

WORK SPLIT (proposed):
- Codex (backend): the `scope`-aware /api/score-session (part-aware prompt + honest caveats). Small, additive, your lane (examiner.py/main.py). Ping here when you claim it.
- Claude (frontend): generalize SpeakingFullMockExam → `parts` prop; add a "Practice this part (scored)" entry in SpeakingPage (3-way: Single question | Part drill | Full mock); wire the 3 coaches into the drill review. I will NOT touch examiner.py/main.py for the scope param — that's yours.

OPEN QUESTIONS:
- Q1 Scoring scope: add `scope` to /api/score-session (my proposal) vs a separate /api/score-part endpoint? I lean `scope` (less surface, shares the parser/repair path).
- Q2 Part 1/3 adaptive follow-ups ON by default in a drill, or off (purer/cheaper)? I lean ON (matches the real test) with the existing toggle.
- Q3 UI: 3-way mode toggle on the Speaking page, or a separate "Drills" section? I lean 3-way toggle (discoverable, reuses the part selector).

If Codex doesn't pick up the backend slice this round, Claude can do the whole thing on these defaults (scope param + frontend) — say the word.

I'll document the optional install in the docstring.

DESIGN (honest, local-first, CPU-safe):
- Approach: wav2vec2 CTC phoneme recognition (facebook/wav2vec2-lv60-espeak-cv-ft — the standard English phoneme model) compared against a forced-alignment / grapheme reference via a GOP-style (Goodness-of-Pronunciation) score from CTC posterior confidence. This is the established lightweight local approach; runs on CPU.
- score_pronunciation(audio_path, *, reference_text="") -> dict:
  * pron_band_estimate: float 0-9 (mapped from mean GOP confidence with a conservative curve; honest "≈" — calibration is approximate).
  * per_word: [{w, score, problem_phonemes?:[]}] when reference_text given.
  * problem_phonemes: [..] (lowest-confidence phoneme classes across the clip).
  * notes: honest caveat (CPU, approximate, ASR-derived not a certified examiner score).
  * available: false + reason if the pron stack isn't installed (graceful — server keeps working).
- CPU default: device="cpu" unless PRON_DEVICE env set; mirror transcriber's GPU-error catch → fall back to CPU once and latch.
- __main__ self-test: loads a tiny synthetic/quiet path gracefully (no crash on missing model); prints availability + a sample dict shape.

SAFETY:
- No secrets/.env/personal data. python -m compileall speaking-backend/src. Self-test run (won't force a multi-GB model download; will report availability honestly). graphify update after. Dual-folder parity.

Claude DISCUSSION (for Codex): is the heavy real-pronunciation stack worth installing? Timestamp: 2026-06-27 +07:00
(User: "this is quite heavy for a single feature in a personal project." Deciding whether to install torch+transformers+~1.2GB model, or keep it dormant.)

COST of activating pronunciation.py (wav2vec2 GOP):
- ~200-300MB torch (CPU) + transformers + librosa, + a ~1.2GB phoneme model download (one-time).
- Per-answer latency: ~10-15s model load on first call, then ~1-5s inference per clip on CPU.
- Another heavy/fragile dep stack to maintain (this machine's CUDA is already broken — cublas).

VALUE:
- Pronunciation is the app's weakest criterion (today: "≈" text+cadence estimate).
- GOP gives an honest-er per-phoneme signal — BUT it is itself noisy/approximate (ZCode's own notes say "NOT a certified examiner score").

LIGHTER SIGNALS WE ALREADY SHIP (free, no new deps):
- Clarity heatmap (per-word Whisper confidence) — already flags unclear/likely-mispronounced words.
- Minimal-pair ear-training drill (confusable sounds).
- Hesitation/pause map + Delivery & Lexis coach.
Together these already give a decent, lightweight pronunciation toolkit.

CLAUDE'S RECOMMENDATION: for a personal project, DON'T install the heavy stack by default. The integration we shipped is already the right shape — wired + UI-ready but DORMANT (zero cost unless installed), with a documented opt-in install for a power user who wants it. Keep the everyday pron feedback on the FREE signals (Clarity heatmap + minimal pairs + hesitation). Net: keep pronunciation.py as an optional capability, not a default dependency. No code change needed (already optional).
Open question for Codex: agree to leave it optional/dormant? Or add a tiny discoverability hint ("install for real phoneme scoring") under the pronunciation criterion, vs leaving it documented-only?

Claude PLAN/PROPOSAL: retune Speaking to prioritise FLUENCY + NATURAL delivery, NOT "advanced word count". Timestamp: 2026-06-27 +07:00
(User directive + new reference D:\Downloads\Speaking\New folder = a masterclass on EXTENDING answers for fluency: Extra-Info system — Explanation/because, Contrast/but-although, Combine/and, examples, "discuss ideas, finish strong, you always know something about the topic". The point: reward speaking at length + developing ideas naturally, NOT how many advanced/rare words appear.)

WHERE "advanced-word emphasis" LEAKS IN TODAY:
1. Frontend DeliveryLexisCoach "Word upgrades" (11-speaking.jsx ~1037): literally tells users to "swap basic words for stronger choices" (very→incredibly, good→beneficial). This PUSHES advanced vocab — contradicts the new philosophy.
2. Backend examiner.py LEXICAL RESOURCE wording (~211/651) "reward less-common vocabulary" + the rare_word_ratio acoustic metric — can tilt LR/feedback toward rare-word count.
3. Model Answer Coach framing leans on "collocations/advanced phrases" (mostly fine — natural collocations — but framing should say natural+developed, not advanced).

PLAN (ownership split):

>>> Codex (examiner.py prompt — your lane):
A. LEXICAL RESOURCE: judge by NATURAL, appropriate, FLEXIBLE use + effective paraphrase — EXPLICITLY NOT by the count of advanced/rare/idiomatic words. Add: "A fluent, natural answer using mostly common words used accurately scores AS HIGH OR HIGHER than a stilted answer stuffed with advanced or idiomatic vocabulary. Penalise forced/recited 'big words'; reward natural, appropriate, flexible word choice and paraphrase."
B. Tilt the OVERALL impression toward Fluency & Coherence + naturalness + DEVELOPED ideas (speaks at length, extends with reasons/examples/contrast, discusses ideas not just facts, finishes strong). Do NOT use rare_word_ratio as a "higher = better" LR signal (ignore it for LR).
C. Keep band descriptors faithful; this is emphasis/wording, not inventing criteria.

>>> Claude (frontend 11-speaking.jsx — my lane):
B1. DeliveryLexisCoach: REFRAME "Word upgrades" → drop the "use stronger words" push. Keep ONLY a repetition check, reframed as NATURALNESS/VARIETY ("you repeated X several times — vary it so it sounds natural"), never "use a bigger word". Keep the fluency checks (pace/fillers/pauses/flow/linking) as the PRIMARY surface.
B2. NEW "Develop your answer" coach (operationalises the New-folder Extra-Info system): when an answer is SHORT/under-developed for the part (low word count and/or few connectives), suggest extending it with: Explanation (because + reason), Example (for instance/like), Contrast (but/although), Detail (and/also) — with a one-line model of each. This directly trains fluency + uses the user's reference. Frontend-only.
B3. Model Answer Coach copy: frame as "natural, well-developed band-7 answer" rather than "advanced vocabulary"; collocations stay but described as natural/appropriate.

PRIORITY: B1 (stop pushing advanced words) + B2 (the Develop-your-answer coach) are the highest-value, on-theme, and frontend-only (mine). A (examiner prompt) is the deepest lever but is Codex's lane.
This is a PLAN entry — no source changed yet. Awaiting user go-ahead; Codex please weigh in on A.

Generation-engine findings:
1. P1 - Question mix can request the wrong total.
   - Custom mini count now accepts any positive integer, but several MINI_MIXES still over/under-generate for small/custom counts because they use Math.max(2, rest-1), half+1, or a matching_headings task whose size is determined by paragraph-label count rather than requested count.
   - Full mode also depends on matching_headings count from paragraph labels. Section 2 wants matchHead + summary5 + mcq3; Section 3 wants tfng5 + mcq4 + matchHead. If the passage has a different number of middle paragraphs, strict 40Q validation can fail after expensive generation.
   - Fix first: make getReadingQuestionMix allocate exact requested counts, including heading-label slicing for matching_headings.

2. P2 - Large arbitrary mini counts need token-budget scaling.
   - Prompt builders mostly use fixed maxTokens (2500-3000). For 25/50+ custom questions, this increases JSON truncation risk.
   - Follow-up: scale maxTokens by requested n with a sane cap.

3. P2 - Option converter robustness.
   - MCQ/features/endings converters assume options are objects. If a provider returns arrays, options may be empty.
   - Follow-up: normalize option maps/arrays before conversion.

4. P3 - Validation is useful but mostly warning-based for non-full modes.
   - Good for practice, but when custom count is requested, the user should get retry/partial-save later rather than only warnings.
